King of the English from 975 to 978

**Edward the Martyr** (c. 962 – 18 March 978) was [King of the English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_the_English "King of the English") from 8 July 975 until he was killed in 978. He was the eldest son of [King Edgar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar,_King_of_England "Edgar, King of England") (r. 959–975). On Edgar's death, the succession to the throne was contested between Edward's supporters and those of his younger half-brother, the future King [Æthelred the Unready](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelred_the_Unready "Æthelred the Unready"). As they were both children, it is unlikely that they played an active role in the dispute, which was probably between rival family alliances. Edward's principal supporters were [Dunstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunstan "Dunstan"), [Archbishop of Canterbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury "Archbishop of Canterbury"), and [Æthelwine, Ealdorman of East Anglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwine,_Ealdorman_of_East_Anglia "Æthelwine, Ealdorman of East Anglia"), while Æthelred was backed by his mother Queen [Ælfthryth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfthryth_\(wife_of_Edgar\) "Ælfthryth (wife of Edgar)") and her friend [Æthelwold, Bishop of Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwold,_Bishop_of_Winchester "Æthelwold, Bishop of Winchester"). The dispute was quickly settled. Edward was chosen as king and Æthelred received the lands traditionally allocated to the king's eldest son in compensation.

Edgar had been a strong and overbearing king and a supporter of the [monastic reform movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Benedictine_Reform "English Benedictine Reform"). He had forced the lay nobility and [secular clergy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_clergy "Secular clergy") to surrender land and sell it at low prices to the monasteries. Æthelwold had been the most active and ruthless in seizing land for his monasteries with Edgar's assistance. The nobles took advantage of Edgar's death to get their lands back, mainly by legal actions but sometimes by force. The leading magnates were split into two factions, the supporters of [Ælfhere, Ealdorman of Mercia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfhere,_Ealdorman_of_Mercia "Ælfhere, Ealdorman of Mercia"), and Æthelwine, who both seized some monastic lands which they believed belonged to them, but also estates claimed by their rivals. The disputes never led to warfare.

Edward's short reign was brought to an end by his murder in March 978 in unclear circumstances. He was killed on his step-mother Ælfthryth's estate at [the Gap of Corfe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corfe_Castle_\(village\) "Corfe Castle (village)") in [Dorset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorset "Dorset"), and hurriedly buried at [Wareham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wareham,_Dorset "Wareham, Dorset"). A year later, his body was [translated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Translation_\(relic\) "Translation (relic)") with great ceremony to [Shaftesbury Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaftesbury_Abbey "Shaftesbury Abbey") in Dorset. Contemporary writers do not name the murderer, but almost all narratives in the period after the [Norman Conquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Conquest "Norman Conquest") name Ælfthryth. Some modern historians agree, but others do not. Another theory is that the killers were [thegns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thegn "Thegn") of Æthelred, probably acting without orders.

Medieval kings were believed to be [sacrosanct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrosanct "Sacrosanct"), and Edward's murder deeply troubled contemporaries who regarded it as a mortal sin. He soon came to be revered as a saint, and his feast of 18 March is listed in the [festal calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calendar_of_saints "Calendar of saints") of the *[Book of Common Prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Common_Prayer_\(1662\) "Book of Common Prayer (1662)")* of the [Church of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_England "Church of England"). Edward was known in his own time for physical and verbal abuse of his associates and companions, and historians consider his veneration thoroughly undeserved.

## Background

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Background")\]

In the ninth century, [Anglo-Saxon England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Saxon_England "Anglo-Saxon England") came under increasing attack from [Viking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viking "Viking") raids, culminating with an invasion by the Viking [Great Heathen Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Heathen_Army "Great Heathen Army") in 865. By 878, the Vikings had overrun the kingdoms of [Northumbria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northumbria "Northumbria"), [East Anglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_East_Anglia "Kingdom of East Anglia"), and [Mercia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercia "Mercia"), and nearly conquered [Wessex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wessex "Wessex"), but in that year the West Saxons under King [Alfred the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_the_Great "Alfred the Great") (871–899) achieved a decisive victory at the [Battle of Edington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Edington "Battle of Edington").<sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynesLapidge19839,_12–13_9-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynesLapidge19839, 12–13&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynesLapidge19839,_12–13-9&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1983&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;9, 12–13&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynesLapidge19839,_12–13-9" id="mwbA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbg">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbw">]</span></span></a></sup> Over the next fifty years, the West Saxons and Mercians gradually conquered the Viking-ruled areas, and in 927 Alfred's grandson [Æthelstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelstan "Æthelstan") (924–939) became the first king of all England when he conquered Northumbria.<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiller2011Foot2011_10-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2011Foot2011-10" id="mwcQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdA">]</span></span></a></sup> He was succeeded by his half-brother and Edward's grandfather, [Edmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_I "Edmund I"), who almost immediately lost control of the north to the Vikings, but recovered full control of England by 944. He was killed in a brawl with an outlaw in 946, and as his sons [Eadwig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eadwig "Eadwig") and [Edgar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar,_King_of_England "Edgar, King of England") were infants, their uncle [Eadred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eadred "Eadred") (946–955) became king.<sup about="#mwt45" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004a_11-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2004a&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004a-11&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004a&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a id="mwfA">]</span></span></a></sup> Like Edmund, Eadred inherited the kingship of the whole of England and soon lost it when [York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavian_York "Scandinavian York") (southern Northumbria) accepted a Viking king, but he recovered it when the York magnates expelled King [Erik Bloodaxe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erik_Bloodaxe "Erik Bloodaxe") in 954.<sup about="#mwt48" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiller2014a154–155_12-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMiller2014a154–155&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014a154–155-12&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014a&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;154–155&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014a154–155-12" id="mwfw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgQ">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgg">]</span></span></a></sup>

Eadred's key advisers included [Dunstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunstan "Dunstan"), [Abbot of Glastonbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glastonbury_Abbey "Glastonbury Abbey") and future [Archbishop of Canterbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury "Archbishop of Canterbury"). Eadred, who suffered from ill health, was in his early thirties when he died in 955, and Eadwig succeeded at the age of around fifteen.<sup about="#mwt51" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004bKeynes2004_13-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2004bKeynes2004&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004bKeynes2004-13&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004b&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004bKeynes2004-13" id="mwhw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwiA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwig">]</span></span></a></sup> He was the first king since the early ninth century not to face the threat of imminent foreign invasion, and England remained free from Viking attacks until 980, after Edward's death.<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStenton1971364_14-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStenton1971364&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971364-14&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stenton&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;364&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971364-14" id="mwiw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjQ">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjg">]</span></span></a></sup> From the start, Eadwig demonstrated his determination to establish his independence from his uncle's advisers. He clashed with Dunstan and sent him into exile. In 957, the kingdom was divided between Eadwig, who kept Wessex, and Edward's father Edgar, who became king of Mercia and other lands north of the [Thames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thames "Thames"). It is unclear whether this had been planned since the beginning of his reign or was the result of a successful revolt brought about by Eadwig's incompetence.<sup about="#mwt57" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2004_15-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2004&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2004-15&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2004-15" id="mwkA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwkQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkg">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkw">]</span></span></a></sup>

Eadwig died in 959, and Edgar succeeded to the rule of the whole kingdom.<sup about="#mwt60" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014_16-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2014&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014-16&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014-16" id="mwlQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlw">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmA">]</span></span></a></sup> Eadwig had appointed [Ælfhere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfhere "Ælfhere") to be [ealdorman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ealdorman "Ealdorman")<sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-18&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ealdorman was the second rank of the lay aristocracy below the king. They governed large areas as the king's local representatives and led local levies in battle.{{sfn|Stafford|2014|p=156}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-18" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwmw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnQ">[</span>c<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwng">]</span></span></a></sup> of Mercia, and he became the premier layman, a status he retained until his death in 983. His rise was at the expense of the family of [Æthelstan Half-King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelstan_Half-King "Æthelstan Half-King"),<sup about="#mwt69" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-20&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Æthelstan was known as the Half-King because kings were said to rely on his advice. He retired in 957 and was succeeded as ealdorman of East Anglia by his eldest son, [[Æthelwald, Ealdorman of East Anglia|Æthelwold]], Ælfthryth's first husband, who died in 962. Æthelwold was succeeded as ealdorman by his youngest brother, [[Æthelwine, Ealdorman of East Anglia|Æthelwine]].{{sfnm|1a1=Miller|1y=2014d|1p=19|2a1=Lapidge|2y=2009|2pp=84–87|3a1=Lapidge|3y=2014|3p=20}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-20" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwoA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwoQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwog">[</span>d<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwow">]</span></span></a></sup> Ealdorman of East Anglia, leading to a rivalry between the families which disrupted the country in Edward's reign.<sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c_21-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c-21&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004c&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c-21" id="mwpA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwpQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpw">]</span></span></a></sup> The [Benedictine reform movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Benedictine_Reform "English Benedictine Reform") reached its peak in Edgar's reign under the leadership of Dunstan, [Oswald, Archbishop of York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oswald,_Archbishop_of_York "Oswald, Archbishop of York"), and [Æthelwold, Bishop of Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwold,_Bishop_of_Winchester "Æthelwold, Bishop of Winchester"). It became dominant as a result of the strong support of Edgar, earning him high praise by contemporary and later monastic chroniclers. He was a strong, indeed overbearing ruler, and he enriched Benedictine monasteries by forcing the aristocracy and secular (non-monastic) religious institutions to surrender land to them. Æthelwold was the most active and ruthless of the Benedictine leaders in securing land to support his monasteries, in some cases driving [secular clergy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_clergy "Secular clergy") out of their establishments in favour of monks. Edgar died at the age of only thirty-one or thirty-two in 975.<sup about="#mwt78" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Yorke2004aMiller2014b163–164_22-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Yorke2004aMiller2014b163–164&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Yorke2004aMiller2014b163–164-22&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004a&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014b&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;163–164&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Yorke2004aMiller2014b163–164-22" id="mwrA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrw">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Family

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Family")\]

Edward, who was born in around 962, was the eldest of the four known children of King Edgar.<sup about="#mwt81" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart2007_23-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart2007&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23" id="mwsg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtQ">]</span></span></a></sup> No contemporary source gives the name of Edward's mother, and post-Conquest sources give varying accounts. The earliest is a life of Dunstan by [Osbern of Canterbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osbern_of_Canterbury "Osbern of Canterbury"), written around 1090. He wrote that Edward's mother was a nun at [Wilton Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilton_Abbey "Wilton Abbey") whom the King seduced, and Dunstan punished Edgar by imposing a seven-year penance which delayed his coronation. Osbern's account is rejected by later chroniclers and modern historians. When [Eadmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eadmer "Eadmer") wrote a life of Dunstan in the early twelfth century, he included an account of Edward's parentage which he obtained from his friend [Nicholas of Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_of_Worcester "Nicholas of Worcester"), who consulted ancient chronicles. Nicholas said that Edward was the son of Edgar's lawful wife [Æthelflæd *candida*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelflæd_Eneda "Æthelflæd Eneda") (the white), daughter of Ealdorman Ordmær.<sup about="#mwt84" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033,_157_n._14TurnerMuir2006lxvii,_136–137_and_n._122_24-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20033, 157 n. 14TurnerMuir2006lxvii, 136–137 and n. 122&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033,_157_n._14TurnerMuir2006lxvii,_136–137_and_n._122-24&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;3, 157 n. 14&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Turner&quot;},&quot;2a2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Muir&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2006&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;lxvii, 136–137 and n. 122&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033,_157_n._14TurnerMuir2006lxvii,_136–137_and_n._122-24" id="mwvA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvg">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvw">]</span></span></a></sup> No ealdorman with that name is known, but some historians identify Æthelflæd's parents as a *vir potens* (powerful man) called Ordmær and his wife Ealde, who gave [Hatfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatfield,_Hertfordshire "Hatfield, Hertfordshire") in [Hertfordshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hertfordshire "Hertfordshire") to Æthelstan Half-King in exchange for land in Devon.<sup about="#mwt87" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart1992586_25-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart1992586&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992586-25&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1992&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;586&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992586-25" id="mwww"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxQ">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">]</span></span></a></sup> John of Worcester, writing in the early twelfth century, also said that Edward was the son of Æthelflæd, adding that she had the byname *eneda* (duck).<sup about="#mwt90" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033DarlingtonMcGurk1995416–417_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20033DarlingtonMcGurk1995416–417&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033DarlingtonMcGurk1995416–417-26&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;3&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Darlington&quot;},&quot;2a2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;McGurk&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1995&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;416–417&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20033DarlingtonMcGurk1995416–417-26" id="mwyA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyg">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyw">]</span></span></a></sup> Edgar, who was brought up by Æthelstan Half-King's wife [Ælfwynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfwynn,_wife_of_Æthelstan_Half-King "Ælfwynn, wife of Æthelstan Half-King"), probably met Æthelflæd as a result of the connection between her father and his foster-father.<sup about="#mwt93" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Hart1992586_27-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Hart1992586&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Hart1992586-27&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1992&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;586&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2014Hart1992586-27" id="mwzQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzw">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt96" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-29&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;The twelfth century ''[[Liber Eliensis]]'' states that Edgar later claimed that Ordmær and Ealde bequeathed the land at Hatfield to him and gave the Hatfield land to Ely Abbey. After his death Æthelstan's sons claimed that Edgar had forced him to surrender it. They brought a successful action to recover the land from the abbey.{{sfnm|1a1=Hart|1y=1992|1pp=462–463, 586|2a1=Fairweather|2y=2005|2pp=103–104}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-29" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mw0Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw0g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0w">[</span>e<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1A">]</span></span></a></sup> Almost all modern historians who have discussed Edward's parentage say that his mother was Æthelflæd,<sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-31" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-31&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;[[Ann Williams (historian)|Ann Williams]] disputes the consensus, suggesting that Edward was a son of Wulfthryth and thus a full brother of Edith. She thinks that Æthelflæd was invented to absolve Edgar from Osbern's charge that he seduced a nun.{{sfn|Williams|2003|pp=6, 159 n. 33}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-31" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mw1Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw1g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1w">[</span>f<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2A">]</span></span></a></sup> and most think that she was Edgar's first wife.<sup about="#mwt108" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201643Yorke2008144_32-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201643Yorke2008144&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201643Yorke2008144-32&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;43&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;144&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201643Yorke2008144-32" id="mw2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2w">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3A">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian [Nicholas Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Brooks_\(historian\) "Nicholas Brooks (historian)") argues that Edgar must have married Æthelflæd because Dunstan backed Edward's succession to the throne, and he was a strong opponent of irregular unions who would not have supported the claim of an illegitimate son.<sup about="#mwt111" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrooks1984249–250_33-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEBrooks1984249–250&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrooks1984249–250-33&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Brooks&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1984&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;249–250&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrooks1984249–250-33" id="mw3g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw3w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4A">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4Q">]</span></span></a></sup> However, the historian [Cyril Hart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyril_Roy_Hart "Cyril Roy Hart") describes Edward as "of doubtful legitimacy". Æthelflæd probably died shortly after his birth.<sup about="#mwt114" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart2007_23-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart2007&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23" id="mw4w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw5A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5Q">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5g">]</span></span></a></sup> Edward's tutor in religion was [Sideman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sideman_\(bishop\) "Sideman (bishop)"), [Bishop of Crediton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Crediton "Bishop of Crediton").<sup about="#mwt117" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–139_34-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–139&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–139-34&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;138–139&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–139-34" id="mw6Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6w">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7A">]</span></span></a></sup>

Edgar had his only known daughter, [Edith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edith_of_Wilton "Edith of Wilton"), by his second known consort, [Wulfthryth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wulfthryth_of_Wilton "Wulfthryth of Wilton"), who retired shortly afterwards to Wilton Abbey with her child.<sup about="#mwt120" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke2004b_35-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke2004b&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2004b-35&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004b&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2004b-35" id="mw8A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8g">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8w">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt123" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-38" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-38&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Historians do not agree on Wulfthryth's status. [[Rosalind Love]] refers to her as a concubine,{{sfn|Love|2014|p=154}} whereas her status as wife is accepted by [[Pauline Stafford]] and defended by [[Barbara Yorke]].{{sfnm|1a1=Stafford|1y=1989|1p=51|2a1=Yorke|2y=2003|2pp=97–113}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-38" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mw9A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw9Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9g">[</span>g<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9w">]</span></span></a></sup> In 964, Edgar married [Ælfthryth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfthryth_\(wife_of_Edgar\) "Ælfthryth (wife of Edgar)"), the widow of Æthelstan Half-King's eldest son, [Æthelwold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwald,_Ealdorman_of_East_Anglia "Æthelwald, Ealdorman of East Anglia"). Her father was [Ordgar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordgar "Ordgar"), a leading Devonshire [thegn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thegn "Thegn") who was appointed an ealdorman in the same year.<sup about="#mwt132" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELewis2004_39-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELewis2004&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis2004-39&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lewis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis2004-39" id="mw_A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_g">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_w">]</span></span></a></sup> She had two sons, Edmund, who died in 971, and the future King [Æthelred the Unready](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelred_the_Unready "Æthelred the Unready"), who was born around 968. She was the only wife of Edgar to be crowned, and she became a powerful political figure, especially in her son's reign.<sup about="#mwt135" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Williams20036_40-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStafford2004Williams20036&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Williams20036-40&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stafford&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;6&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Williams20036-40" id="mwAQE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQM">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQQ">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Disputed succession

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Disputed succession")\]

Edward is first recorded as a witness to the [Winchester New Minster Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Minster_Charter "New Minster Charter") in 966.<sup about="#mwt138" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart2007_23-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart2007&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23" id="mwAQg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQo">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">]</span></span></a></sup> Ælfthryth attested as the "legitimate wife" of the King and Edmund as his "legitimate son", whereas Edward was described as "begotten by the same king". It is uncertain whether this was on the King's instruction, which would indicate that he wished to cut Edward out of the succession, or was ordered by Bishop Æthelwold, who was a friend and ally of Ælfthryth.<sup about="#mwt141" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke2008148–149Rumble200293–94Charter_S_745_41-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke2008148–149Rumble200293–94Charter S 745&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2008148–149Rumble200293–94Charter_S_745-41&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;148–149&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rumble&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2002&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;93–94&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 745&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2008148–149Rumble200293–94Charter_S_745-41" id="mwAQw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQ0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQ4">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQ8">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian [Barbara Yorke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Yorke "Barbara Yorke") sees the denial of Edward's legitimacy as "opportunist special pleading" by Æthelwold.<sup about="#mwt144" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke198886_42-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke198886&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke198886-42&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;86&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke198886-42" id="mwARE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARM">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARQ">]</span></span></a></sup> A genealogy created at Dunstan's Glastonbury Abbey around 969 gives Edward precedence over Edmund and Æthelred.<sup about="#mwt147" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke2008149_43-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke2008149&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2008149-43&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;149&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2008149-43" id="mwARU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARc">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARg">]</span></span></a></sup> When Edgar died on 8 July 975 there was a dispute over the succession, but as Edward was around thirteen and Æthelred six to nine, the historian Sean Miller observes that "they were surely figureheads rather than active participants".<sup about="#mwt150" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart2007Miller2014c167_44-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart2007Miller2014c167&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007Miller2014c167-44&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;167&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007Miller2014c167-44" id="mwARk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARs">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARw">]</span></span></a></sup>

Some historians have seen Edward's supporters as defenders of monastic reform and Æthelred's as its opponents, but there were supporters on both sides, and this view is now generally rejected. The real reasons for choosing between them probably lay in family alliances. Bishop Æthelwold backed his friend Ælfthryth, who naturally put forward the claim of her son Æthelred, while Archbishop Dunstan was Edward's chief supporter.<sup about="#mwt153" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFisher1952254–255Miller2014c167Williams20039_45-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFisher1952254–255Miller2014c167Williams20039&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFisher1952254–255Miller2014c167Williams20039-45&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Fisher&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1952&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;254–255&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;167&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;3p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;9&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFisher1952254–255Miller2014c167Williams20039-45" id="mwAR4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASA">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASE">]</span></span></a></sup> According to John of Worcester, Archbishop Oswald also supported Edward.<sup about="#mwt156" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDarlingtonMcGurk1995426–427_46-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEDarlingtonMcGurk1995426–427&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEDarlingtonMcGurk1995426–427-46&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Darlington&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;McGurk&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1995&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;426–427&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDarlingtonMcGurk1995426–427-46" id="mwASI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASQ">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASU">]</span></span></a></sup> Oswald was at odds with Ealdorman Ælfhere, who probably supported Æthelred,<sup about="#mwt159" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-48" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-48&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;In his will, Ælfhere's brother Ælfheah described Ælfthryth as his ''gefædera'', a word which denotes the relationship between a parent and a godparent or godparents of the same child. He made bequests to Ælfthryth and her sons, \&quot;the elder ætheling\&quot; and \&quot;the younger ætheling\&quot;. Edward is not mentioned.{{sfnm|1a1=Williams|1y=2003|1pp=2, 9|2a1=Whitelock|2y=1930|2pp=22–23}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-48" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwASY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASg">[</span>h<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASk">]</span></span></a></sup> together with his brother-in-law [Ælfric Cild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfric_Cild "Ælfric Cild"), while Ælfhere's enemy [Æthelwine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwine,_Ealdorman_of_East_Anglia "Æthelwine, Ealdorman of East Anglia") (son of Æthelstan Half-King) probably backed Edward.<sup about="#mwt165" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039_49-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20039&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039-49&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;9&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039-49" id="mwASw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS4">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">]</span></span></a></sup>

Byrhtferth's life of Oswald portrays Edward as an unstable and violent young man:

> Now certain of the magnates of this realm wished to elect the elder son of King Edgar, named Edward, as king; some of the ealdormen wanted the younger son, because he seemed more gentle to everyone in word and deed. But the elder son struck not only fear but even terror into everyone; he hounded them not only with tongue-lashings but even with cruel beatings – and most of all those who were members of his own household."<sup about="#mwt170" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatson20212Lapidge2009136–139_50-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWatson20212Lapidge2009136–139&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20212Lapidge2009136–139-50&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20212Lapidge2009136–139-50" id="mwATM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATU">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATY">]</span></span></a></sup>

The historian [Frank Stenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Stenton "Frank Stenton") comments in his "magisterial and massively authoritative" *Anglo-Saxon England*:<sup about="#mwt172" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2003xxi_51-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2003xxi&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2003xxi-51&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;xxi&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2003xxi-51" id="mwATo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATw">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT0">]</span></span></a></sup> "Long after \[Edward\] had passed into veneration as a saint it was remembered that his outbursts of rage had alarmed all who knew him  ... It may have been partly for this reason that a large number of nobles resolved to promote the election of Æthelred, the younger brother."<sup about="#mwt176" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStenton1971372_52-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStenton1971372&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971372-52&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stenton&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;372&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971372-52" id="mwAT8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUE">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUI">]</span></span></a></sup>

The Benedictine monk [Eadmer of Canterbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eadmer_of_Canterbury "Eadmer of Canterbury") wrote in his [hagiographical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hagiographical "Hagiographical") life of Dunstan:

> When this Edward ought to have been consecrated as king, many of the princes of the land sought to oppose that he should be made king, on the one hand, because they were wary of the severity of his character with which he had customarily criticized the excesses of his men fiercely and on the other because they knew that his mother, though legally married, had not, however, been consecrated to the kingdom – just as his father had not – when she gave birth to him.<sup about="#mwt181" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETurnerMuir2006144–145_53-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTETurnerMuir2006144–145&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTETurnerMuir2006144–145-53&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurnerMuir2006144–145-53" id="mwAUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUo">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUs">]</span></span></a></sup>

The historian [Ann Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Williams_\(historian\) "Ann Williams (historian)") is sceptical of the last point, commenting that while it is possible that consecration of the king's wife before she gave birth may have been an issue in the tenth century, Eadmer was writing in the early twelfth century when it was an argument raised in favour of King [Henry I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_I_of_England "Henry I of England") against his elder brother, and this may have influenced his interpretation. In addition, as Eadmer says that Edgar was not crowned until two years before his death, the same argument applied to Æthelred.<sup about="#mwt183" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039_49-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20039&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039-49&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;9&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20039-49" id="mwAU8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVE">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVI">]</span></span></a></sup>

The dispute was soon settled. A [calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calendar_of_saints "Calendar of saints") entry in a manuscript dating to the late tenth or early eleventh century gives the date of Edward's election as king as 19 July, less than a fortnight after Edgar's death.<sup about="#mwt186" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012b136–137Keynes1980239_n._22Keynes2013150_54-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2012b136–137Keynes1980239 n. 22Keynes2013150&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012b136–137Keynes1980239_n._22Keynes2013150-54&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012b&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;136–137&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;239 n. 22&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2013&quot;},&quot;3p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;150&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012b136–137Keynes1980239_n._22Keynes2013150-54" id="mwAVU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVc">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVg">]</span></span></a></sup> A charter probably dating to 999 states that Edward was unanimously chosen as king by the leading men of both orders. Æthelred was given the lands traditionally held by the king's sons, including some which had been granted by Edgar to [Abingdon Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abingdon_Abbey "Abingdon Abbey"), and which were now forcibly repossessed by the order of all the leading nobles. Æthelred commented in the charter that "whether they did this thing justly or unjustly, they themselves may know", and he granted other lands to Abingdon in compensation. The charter probably reflects a settlement under which Æthelred's supporters agreed to Edward's succession in return for the land grant.<sup about="#mwt189" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310Charter_S_937Whitelock1979582–584_(no._123)_3-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200310Charter S 937Whitelock1979582–584 (no. 123)&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310Charter_S_937Whitelock1979582–584_(no._123)-3&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 937&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;582–584 (no. 123)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310Charter_S_937Whitelock1979582–584_(no._123)-3" id="mwAVo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAV0">]</span></span></a></sup> He was crowned by Archbishop Dunstan at [Kingston upon Thames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston_upon_Thames "Kingston upon Thames"), possibly on the same day he was elected.<sup about="#mwt192" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2013148,_150_55-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2013148, 150&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2013148,_150-55&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2013&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;148, 150&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2013148,_150-55" id="mwAV8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWI">]</span></span></a></sup>

The post-Conquest *Passio* gives a different account, claiming that Dunstan forced through the coronation of Edward as king: "But when, at the time of \[Edward's\] consecration, some of the leading men of the nation had wished to oppose \[it\], St Dunstan persevered single-mindedly in his election, and, taking hold of the banner of the holy cross which was customarily carried before him, he fixed it upright in the middle, and with the remaining pious bishops consecrated him king."<sup about="#mwt195" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECubitt2008145_56-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTECubitt2008145&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTECubitt2008145-56&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Cubitt&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;145&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECubitt2008145-56" id="mwAWU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWc">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWg">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Edward's reign

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Edward's reign")\]

### The "anti-monastic reaction"

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: The \"anti-monastic reaction\"")\]

Edward's succession was followed by what historians sometimes call the "anti-monastic reaction".<sup about="#mwt198" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge2009xx,_lxxi,_lxxiiiMiller2014c167Williams200310_57-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge2009xx, lxxi, lxxiiiMiller2014c167Williams200310&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009xx,_lxxi,_lxxiiiMiller2014c167Williams200310-57&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;xx, lxxi, lxxiii&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;167&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;3p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009xx,_lxxi,_lxxiiiMiller2014c167Williams200310-57" id="mwAWw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAW0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW4">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW8">]</span></span></a></sup> According to the *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle*, Ælfhere and many other nobles, described by *ASC D*<sup about="#mwt201" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-59&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Manuscripts of the ''Anglo-Saxon Chronicle'' are conventionally labelled ''ASC A'' to ''ASC F''.{{sfn|Whitelock|1979|pp=109–117}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-59" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwAXI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXQ">[</span>i<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXU">]</span></span></a></sup> as the "adversaries of God", launched attacks on monasteries. His rival Æthelwine was called *amicus Dei* (friend of God), and portrayed as the chief defender of the monks, by Byrhtferth,<sup about="#mwt207" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979229Lapidge2009130–131_60-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979229Lapidge2009130–131&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979229Lapidge2009130–131-60&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;229&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;130–131&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979229Lapidge2009130–131-60" id="mwAXc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXk">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXo">]</span></span></a></sup> who wrote that in Edward's reign:

> the commonwealth of the entire realm was shaken; bishops were perplexed, ealdormen were angry, monks were struck with fear, the people were terrified, and the secular clerics were made happy, because their time had come. Abbots are now expelled, together with their monks; clerics are brought in together with their wives; and 'the last error was worse than the first' ... Monks who were formerly accustomed to sit on [caparisoned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caparison "Caparison") horses and with their companions to sing the melodious song of [King David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_David "King David"), could then be seen carting a burden, not being carried like the ancient patriarch on a carriage into Egypt, or walking about with companions and friends, 'without a purse, without shoes'.<sup about="#mwt212" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge2009122–125_61-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge2009122–125&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009122–125-61&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009122–125-61" id="mwAX0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAX4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAX8">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYA">]</span></span></a></sup>

However, most historians are sceptical of the claim that the conflict was between the supporters and opponents of monasticism, and attribute the disturbances to the nobles' personal rivalries and their determination to recover or obtain compensation for lands which Edgar had forced them to give up to monasteries.<sup about="#mwt214" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004cMiller2014c168Jayakumar2009339–340_62-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2004cMiller2014c168Jayakumar2009339–340&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004cMiller2014c168Jayakumar2009339–340-62&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004c&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;168&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Jayakumar&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;339–340&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004cMiller2014c168Jayakumar2009339–340-62" id="mwAYI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYQ">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYU">]</span></span></a></sup> Æthelwine founded Byrhtferth's [Ramsey Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramsey_Abbey "Ramsey Abbey"), but he was remembered at [Ely Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ely_Abbey "Ely Abbey") for stealing several of their estates.<sup about="#mwt217" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168_63-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168-63&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;168&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168-63" id="mwAYg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYo">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYs">]</span></span></a></sup> Ælfhere is said to have disbanded monasteries founded by Æthelwold, but also to have protected Æthelwold's Ely from Æthelwine and been a benefactor of Dunstan's Glastonbury and Æthelwold's Abingdon.<sup about="#mwt220" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c_21-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c-21&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004c&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2004c-21" id="mwAYw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAY0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY4">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY8">]</span></span></a></sup> Williams comments that "there was more to the seizure of monastic lands than anti-monasticism. In many, perhaps most cases, it was the sharp practice involved in acquiring lands for the reformed houses that was being questioned, as the sellers (who had probably been put under considerable pressure) or their heirs sought to obtain a price closer to the actual market value."<sup about="#mwt223" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310_64-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200310&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310-64&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310-64" id="mwAZA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZI">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZM">]</span></span></a></sup>

Historians find it very difficult to judge the extent of disorder in Edward's reign in view of the very limited information available. At some point, Ælfhere and Æthelwine appear to have come close to open warfare, but there were no battles.<sup about="#mwt226" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310–11_65-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200310–11&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310–11-65&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10–11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200310–11-65" id="mwAZU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZc">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZg">]</span></span></a></sup> A thegn called Leofsige seized estates at [Peterborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterborough "Peterborough"), [Oundle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oundle "Oundle") and [Kettering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kettering "Kettering"), causing such disorder that no crops were sown for two years. Leofsige was killed by Æthelwine's brother, Ælfwold, who was then pardoned for the crime by Bishop Æthelwold.<sup about="#mwt229" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart1992151_66-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart1992151&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992151-66&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1992&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;151&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992151-66" id="mwAZw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZ0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ4">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ8">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian George Molyneaux is sceptical of the portrayal of Edward's reign as a time of rampant strife in view of the lack of evidence of fighting.<sup about="#mwt232" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMolyneaux201534_67-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMolyneaux201534&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMolyneaux201534-67&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Molyneaux&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2015&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;34&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMolyneaux201534-67" id="mwAaA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaI">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaM">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Administration

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Administration")\]

*[Witans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witan "Witan")* (king's councils) met at Kingston upon Thames, perhaps on the occasion of Edward's coronation, at [Kirtlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtlington "Kirtlington") in [Oxfordshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfordshire "Oxfordshire") after Easter 977, and at [Calne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calne "Calne") in [Wiltshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiltshire "Wiltshire") in the same year. The meeting at Calne was held on an upper storey which collapsed; many leading counsellors were injured and some killed, but Dunstan was unharmed because he was standing on a beam.<sup about="#mwt235" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201367,_240Williams200311_68-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201367, 240Williams200311&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201367,_240Williams200311-68&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2013&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;67,
