240&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201367,_240Williams200311-68" id="mwAaw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAa0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa4">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa8">]</span></span></a></sup> Ælfhere attested first among the ealdormen and Æthelwine second in all of Edward's charters, indicating that they retained their positions from the previous reign as the leading lay magnates.<sup about="#mwt238" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853Hart2005Keynes2002Tables_LVI_(3_of_3),_LVIII_69-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes200853Hart2005Keynes2002Tables LVI (3 of 3), LVIII&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853Hart2005Keynes2002Tables_LVI_(3_of_3),_LVIII-69&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;53&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2005&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2002&quot;},&quot;3loc&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Tables LVI (3 of 3), LVIII&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853Hart2005Keynes2002Tables_LVI_(3_of_3),_LVIII-69" id="mwAbA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbI">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbM">]</span></span></a></sup> Edward's first charter was attested by almost all the leading magnates, showing that his rule had been generally accepted. This is the first charter which lists the chronicler [Æthelweard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelweard_the_Chronicler "Æthelweard the Chronicler") as an ealdorman and he was presumably appointed by Edward.<sup about="#mwt241" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201668–70_70-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201668–70&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201668–70-70&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;68–70&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201668–70-70" id="mwAbU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbc">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbg">]</span></span></a></sup> There appear to have been no ealdormen covering the south of England in the early 970s, perhaps because Edgar's authority was so great that he was able to cover the area personally. After Edward acceded, three ealdormen were appointed to cover the area, Æthelweard in west Wessex, Æthelmær in Hampshire and [Eadwine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eadwine_of_Sussex "Eadwine of Sussex") in Sussex and Kent. If stability had depended on Edgar's personal authority, then it is not surprising that the authorities found it so difficult to maintain order after his death.<sup about="#mwt244" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853_71-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes200853&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853-71&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;53&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200853-71" id="mwAbo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbw">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb0">]</span></span></a></sup> The *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle* disapprovingly records the exile of Ealdorman [Oslac of York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslac_of_York "Oslac of York") during Edward's reign, but the circumstances are unknown. Few other events are recorded during his reign: there was a severe famine in 976, Bishop Sideman died in 977,<sup about="#mwt247" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200311Whitelock1979229–230_72-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200311Whitelock1979229–230&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200311Whitelock1979229–230-72&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;11&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;229–230&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200311Whitelock1979229–230-72" id="mwAcA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcI">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcM">]</span></span></a></sup> and in 978 Dunstan purchased land near [Hendon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendon "Hendon") in [Middlesex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlesex "Middlesex") from Edward.<sup about="#mwt250" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes200557Charter_S_1450_73-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes200557Charter S 1450&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200557Charter_S_1450-73&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2005&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;57&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 1450&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes200557Charter_S_1450-73" id="mwAcY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcg">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAck">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Charters

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Charters")\]

The historian [Simon Keynes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Keynes "Simon Keynes") sees a [diplomatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic "Diplomatic") tradition which extends from the charters late in Edgar's reign to early in Æthelred's, but excluding the intervening "slightly anomalous" charters of Edward. Five charters survive which purport to date to the three years and nine months of Edward's reign, a low number as the average in the period was four to five a year. The authenticity of Charters S 828 and S 829 is disputed. Keynes says that they are fraudulent, but have witness lists which are probably based on genuine texts.<sup about="#mwt253" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_and_n._1Keynes2002Table_LVIIIHart197526–27_74-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes198084 and n. 1Keynes2002Table LVIIIHart197526–27&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_and_n._1Keynes2002Table_LVIIIHart197526–27-74&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;84 and n. 1&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2002&quot;},&quot;2loc&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Table LVIII&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1975&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;26–27&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_and_n._1Keynes2002Table_LVIIIHart197526–27-74" id="mwAc4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAc8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdA">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdE">]</span></span></a></sup> The editor of these charters, Susan Kelly, describes them as "very difficult documents", and thinks that they may be genuine. They both appear to have been drawn up at the same time by the same careless draftsman during or close to Edward's reign. S 828 is a grant of thirteen [hides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hide_\(unit\) "Hide (unit)") of land at [Kingston Bagpuize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston_Bagpuize "Kingston Bagpuize") in [Berkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkshire "Berkshire") by King Edward to Bishop [Ælfstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfstan "Ælfstan"), probably of [Ramsbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Ramsbury_\(ancient\) "Bishop of Ramsbury (ancient)"). S 829 grants seven hides in the same village to [Abingdon Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abingdon_Abbey "Abingdon Abbey"). The boundary clause is the same in both charters, perhaps because the bishop and abbey held different scattered units within the estate.<sup about="#mwt256" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly2001454–456Charter_S_828Charter_S_829_75-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKelly2001454–456Charter S 828Charter S 829&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly2001454–456Charter_S_828Charter_S_829-75&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Kelly&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2001&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;454–456&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 828&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 829&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly2001454–456Charter_S_828Charter_S_829-75" id="mwAdg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdo">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAds">]</span></span></a></sup> Charter S 831 from the [Old Minster, Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Minster,_Winchester "Old Minster, Winchester"), is probably authentic and is based on one of 941. It is a grant by Edward to his minister Ælfric of land at [Wylye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wylye "Wylye") in [Wiltshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiltshire "Wiltshire").<sup about="#mwt259" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_n._1Hart197527_n._1Charter_S_831_76-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes198084 n. 1Hart197527 n. 1Charter S 831&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_n._1Hart197527_n._1Charter_S_831-76&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;84 n. 1&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1975&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;27 n. 1&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 831&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes198084_n._1Hart197527_n._1Charter_S_831-76" id="mwAd8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeE">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeI">]</span></span></a></sup> Charter S 832 is a large grant of land in Cornwall by Edward to Ealdorman Æthelweard.<sup about="#mwt262" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979566–567_(no._115,_translation_of_charter)Charter_S_832_77-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979566–567 (no. 115, translation of charter)Charter S 832&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979566–567_(no._115,_translation_of_charter)Charter_S_832-77&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;566–567 (no. 115, translation of charter)&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 832&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979566–567_(no._115,_translation_of_charter)Charter_S_832-77" id="mwAeM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeU">[</span>68<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeY">]</span></span></a></sup> It is closely related to Charter S 830, a grant of land at [Cheriton Bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheriton_Bishop "Cheriton Bishop") in Devon by Edward to his "faithful vassal" (*fideli vasallo*) Ælfsige. Both charters are from the [Exeter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exeter "Exeter") archive but probably written at [Crediton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crediton "Crediton"), and Hart doubts whether they are genuine in their present form.<sup about="#mwt265" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECharter_S_830Hart197526–27,_n._4Chaplais196615–16_78-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTECharter S 830Hart197526–27, n. 4Chaplais196615–16&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTECharter_S_830Hart197526–27,_n._4Chaplais196615–16-78&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 830&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1975&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;26–27, n. 4&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chaplais&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1966&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;15–16&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECharter_S_830Hart197526–27,_n._4Chaplais196615–16-78" id="mwAes"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAew"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAe0">[</span>69<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAe4">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Coinage

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Coinage")\]

![](//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/41/EdwardMartyr.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)

[Obverse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obverse_and_reverse "Obverse and reverse") of a penny of Edward the Martyr produced at [Stamford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamford,_Lincolnshire "Stamford, Lincolnshire") by the coiner Wulfgar<sup about="#mwt268" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2017732–733_(coin_1777)_79-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2017732–733 (coin 1777)&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017732–733_(coin_1777)-79&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2017&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;732–733 (coin 1777)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017732–733_(coin_1777)-79" id="mwAfY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfg">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfk">]</span></span></a></sup>

The only coin in common use in late Anglo-Saxon England was the silver [penny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_English_penny_\(c._600_–_1066\) "History of the English penny (c. 600 – 1066)").<sup about="#mwt271" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2021386_80-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2021386&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2021386-80&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;386&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2021386-80" id="mwAfw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAf0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAf4">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAf8">]</span></span></a></sup> Until late in Edgar's reign, pennies produced by mints in different towns varied in design, weight and [fineness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fineness "Fineness") (silver content). In the early 970s, he brought in his reform coinage, with a single design and much greater uniformity of weight and fineness. The coins, with a design called the *Small Cross* type, had a left-facing bust of the King on the [obverse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obverse "Obverse") surrounded by a circle with the King's name (+EADGAR REX ANGLOR\[UM\]) around the edge. The reverse had a small cross in the centre with the moneyer's name and mint-place around the edge. Edgar's reform design was the sole coin type produced during Edward's reign, and it was replaced early in Æthelred's reign.<sup about="#mwt274" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2017260–261,_732–733_81-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2017260–261, 732–733&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017260–261,_732–733-81&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2017&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;260–261, 732–733&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017260–261,_732–733-81" id="mwAgM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgU">[</span>72<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgY">]</span></span></a></sup>

Edward's coins were on average slightly lighter than those of Edgar and heavier than those of Æthelred.<sup about="#mwt277" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2017249_82-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2017249&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017249-82&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2017&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;249&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017249-82" id="mwAgg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgo">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgs">]</span></span></a></sup> The fineness was high and uniform following Edgar's reform at 96%, and this was maintained under Edward apart from a few slightly less fine coins produced in Lincoln and York. After Edward's death, fineness became more variable.<sup about="#mwt280" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2017252_83-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2017252&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017252-83&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2017&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;252&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017252-83" id="mwAgw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAg0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAg4">[</span>74<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAg8">]</span></span></a></sup> During Edgar's reign, dies for coins were almost all cut at one centre, probably [Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winchester "Winchester"), and distributed from there to other mints across the kingdom. Under Edward, the centre supplied a far smaller number of mints and it was supplemented by regional die-cutting centres. This may have been due to the difficult political situation in his reign.<sup about="#mwt283" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENaismith2017261_84-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTENaismith2017261&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017261-84&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Naismith&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2017&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;261&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENaismith2017261-84" id="mwAhE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhM">[</span>75<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhQ">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Death

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Death")\]

![](//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Corfe_Castle2.jpg/250px-Corfe_Castle2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)

[Corfe Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corfe_Castle "Corfe Castle") after the [Norman Conquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Conquest "Norman Conquest")

Edward was killed on [Ælfthryth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ælfthryth_\(wife_of_Edgar\) "Ælfthryth (wife of Edgar)")'s estate in [the Gap of Corfe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corfe_Castle_\(village\) "Corfe Castle (village)") in the [Purbeck Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purbeck_Hills "Purbeck Hills") in [Dorset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorset "Dorset") on the evening of 18 March 978.<sup about="#mwt286" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart2007_23-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart2007&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hart&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart2007-23" id="mwAiE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiM">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt289" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-87" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-87&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;The historian [[David Dumville]] argues that Edward was killed on 18 March 979, but most historians give 978,{{sfnm|1a1=Dumville|1y=2007|1pp=269–283|2a1=Keynes|2y=2012b|2p=137|3a1=Marafioti|3y=2014|3p=162 n. 3}} and Levi Roach thinks that there is little doubt that 978 is correct.{{sfn|Roach|2016|pp=72–73}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-87" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwAiU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAic">[</span>j<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAig">]</span></span></a></sup> The only detailed pre-Conquest account is by [Byrhtferth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byrhtferth "Byrhtferth"):

> One day towards evening the remarkable and elected king, seeking the consolations of brotherly love, arrived at the house where his beloved brother was living with the dowager queen, as we have said. The magnates and leading men went to meet him, as was only fitting; he \[the younger son\] remained inside with the dowager queen, his mother. Those magnates had agreed among themselves a wicked plot: they were possessed of so damnable an intention and so murky and diabolical a blindness, that they did not fear to lay hands on God's anointed. Armed men surrounded him on all sides; with them was standing the royal butler, humbly waiting to be of service. The venerable king had with him very few soldiers, since he did not suspect anyone, trusting "in the Lord and in the might of His power". He had been instructed in holy scripture under the tutelage of Bishop Sideman; he was strong in body and sturdy. For when the conspirators surrounded him — and it was just as the Jews once surrounded our Lord — he remained sitting on his horse, fearless. They were seized by a single madness, an equal insanity ... The soldiers laid hold of him: one on his right-hand side drew him towards him, as if he wished to give him a kiss; another grabbed his left side firmly and gave him the death blow. And the King shouted out, as best he could: "What are you doing, breaking my right hand?" And suddenly he fell from his horse, and he was dead. This martyr of God was lifted up by his thegns, and taken to the house of a certain churl, where no [Gregorian chant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_chant "Gregorian chant") and no funereal lament was heard; rather, this distinguished king of the whole country lay covered only by a cheap blanket, awaiting the light of day.<sup about="#mwt300" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–141_88-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–141&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–141-88&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009138–141-88" id="mwAiw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAi0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi4">[</span>78<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt306" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-91" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-91&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-91" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwAjA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjI">[</span>k<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjM">]</span></span></a></sup>

Byrhtferth does not say when and where Edward died, and the oldest surviving version of the *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle* (*ASC A*) just says that he was killed in 978. *ASC C*, written in the 1040s after Edward had come to be seen as a saint, states that he was martyred. The fullest account of his death in the *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle* is in the northern recension, *ASC D* and *ASC E*,<sup about="#mwt310" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-93" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;lower-alpha&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-93&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;efn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Efn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot; ''ASC D'' and ''E'' are together known as the northern recension because they contain material of northern interest not found in other versions.{{sfn|Whitelock|1979|p=113}} &quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-93" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwAjs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAj0">[</span>l<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAj4">]</span></span></a></sup> which post-dates the Conquest. This gives the date and location of his death. No version of the *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle* states who committed the murder, but the northern recension blames his relatives for failing to avenge his death,<sup about="#mwt316" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231Williams200311_94-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231Williams200311&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231Williams200311-94&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;230–231&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231Williams200311-94" id="mwAkA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkI">[</span>82<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkM">]</span></span></a></sup> and a poem about Edward's death dating to around 1000 says: "It is certain that he died through envy, at the hands of his own kin."<sup about="#mwt319" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDumville2007277–280Keynes2012a117_95-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEDumville2007277–280Keynes2012a117&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007277–280Keynes2012a117-95&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Dumville&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;277–280&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012a&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;117&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007277–280Keynes2012a117-95" id="mwAkQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkY">[</span>83<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkc">]</span></span></a></sup>

Pre-Conquest accounts of the murder do not say who was responsible, whereas post-Conquest chroniclers and hagiographers almost all blame Ælfthryth. The *Passio* and John of Worcester claim that she ordered the killing,<sup about="#mwt322" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviRoach201674–76DarlingtonMcGurk1995428–429_96-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFell1971xviRoach201674–76DarlingtonMcGurk1995428–429&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviRoach201674–76DarlingtonMcGurk1995428–429-96&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Fell&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;xvi&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;74–76&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Darlington&quot;},&quot;3a2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;McGurk&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1995&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;428–429&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviRoach201674–76DarlingtonMcGurk1995428–429-96" id="mwAko"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAks"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkw">[</span>84<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAk0">]</span></span></a></sup> while William of Malmesbury has her handing him a drink to distract him and her servant stabbing him, and [Henry of Huntingdon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_of_Huntingdon "Henry of Huntingdon") writes that she killed Edward herself.<sup about="#mwt325" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMynorsThomsonWinterbottom1998264–265_(162.2)Greenway1996324–325_97-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMynorsThomsonWinterbottom1998264–265 (162.2)Greenway1996324–325&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMynorsThomsonWinterbottom1998264–265_(162.2)Greenway1996324–325-97&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Mynors&quot;},&quot;1a2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Thomson&quot;},&quot;1a3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Winterbottom&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1998&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;264–265 (162.2)&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Greenway&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1996&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;324–325&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMynorsThomsonWinterbottom1998264–265_(162.2)Greenway1996324–325-97" id="mwAk8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlE">[</span>85<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlI">]</span></span></a></sup> The *Passio* is so biased against Ælfthryth that it deletes favourable information about her in its sources, such as the *[Regularis Concordia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regularis_Concordia_\(Winchester\) "Regularis Concordia (Winchester)")* of around 973, which laid down rules for monastic life. It gave Ælfthryth the role of protectress of nunneries, but the *Passio* changes the protectress to Edward's mother.<sup about="#mwt328" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviiiKeynes1980172_98-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFell1971xviiiKeynes1980172&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviiiKeynes1980172-98&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Fell&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;xviii&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;172&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xviiiKeynes1980172-98" id="mwAlc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlk">[</span>86<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlo">]</span></span></a></sup>

Modern historians have offered a variety of interpretations of Edward's killing. Some blame Ælfthryth's followers and think that she may have been personally responsible.<sup about="#mwt331" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Higham199714_99-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStafford2004Higham199714&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Higham199714-99&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stafford&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2004&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Higham&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1997&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;14&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2004Higham199714-99" id="mwAlw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAl0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl4">[</span>87<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl8">]</span></span></a></sup> Cnut's biographer, Michael Lawson, thinks that the failure to punish the killers is suspicious and points to Ælfthryth's guilt.<sup about="#mwt334" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELawson201145_100-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELawson201145&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELawson201145-100&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lawson&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2011&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;45&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELawson201145-100" id="mwAmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmI">[</span>88<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmM">]</span></span></a></sup> Roach disagrees, seeing the fact that no contemporary blamed Ælfthryth as significant, and comments that we must be careful not to see conspiracies which did not exist.<sup about="#mwt337" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201675–76_101-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201675–76&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201675–76-101&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;75–76&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201675–76-101" id="mwAmQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmY">[</span>89<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmc">]</span></span></a></sup> The editor of the *Passio*, [Christine Fell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Fell "Christine Fell"), points out that many of the details of Ælfthryth's role appear in the earlier stories of the murders of Anglo-Saxon royals, [Saint Kenelm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Kenelm "Saint Kenelm") by his sister and [Saint Æthelberht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelberht_II_of_East_Anglia "Æthelberht II of East Anglia") by his potential mother-in-law. Fell argues that it was inevitable that Ælfthryth's hagiographical role in the *Passio* would similarly be that of scapegoat.<sup about="#mwt340" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFell19788–11_102-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFell19788–11&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell19788–11-102&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Fell&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1978&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;8–11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell19788–11-102" id="mwAm0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAm4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAm8">[</span>90<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnA">]</span></span></a></sup> Yorke comments that such stories "draw upon hagiographical conventions spiced with traditional beliefs in the enmity of step-mothers for step-children, and should not be taken as reliable accounts of what actually occurred".<sup about="#mwt343" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke1999101_103-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke1999101&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999101-103&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;101&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999101-103" id="mwAnE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnM">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnQ">]</span></span></a></sup>

Another theory is that the murder was the culmination of the conflict between Ælfhere and Æthelwine, and that Ælfhere had Edward killed to preserve his own power and put his own candidate on the throne. The historian Alan Thacker sees Ælfhere's role in the reburial of Edward as an act of [expiation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expiation "Expiation").<sup about="#mwt346" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThacker1996248–249John1996120_104-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEThacker1996248–249John1996120&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEThacker1996248–249John1996120-104&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Thacker&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1996&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;248–249&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;John&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1996&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;120&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThacker1996248–249John1996120-104" id="mwAnc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAng"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnk">[</span>92<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAno">]</span></span></a></sup> However, Roach points out that Byrhtferth praises Ælfhere's role, even though he was hostile to the ealdorman, and would have condemned him if he had been implicated in the crime.<sup about="#mwt349" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201676_105-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201676&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201676-105&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;76&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201676-105" id="mwAns"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn0">[</span>93<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn4">]</span></span></a></sup> The contemporary poem about Edward's death praises Ælfhere's role, describing him as "most worthy leader".<sup about="#mwt352" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDumville2007280_106-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEDumville2007280&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007280-106&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Dumville&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;280&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007280-106" id="mwAn8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoE">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoI">]</span></span></a></sup> Miller thinks that Æthelred's thegns probably acted on their own initiative in the hope of personal advancement.<sup about="#mwt355" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168_63-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168-63&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Miller&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014c&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;168&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014c168-63" id="mwAoM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoU">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoY">]</span></span></a></sup>

Edward's personality may have been a factor in his death, as afterwards the opposing factions were able to reach a compromise.<sup about="#mwt358" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke1999107_107-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke1999107&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999107-107&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;107&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999107-107" id="mwAog"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAok"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoo">[</span>95<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAos">]</span></span></a></sup> There were few changes at court: Ælfthryth and Bishop Æthelwold became more prominent, but Edward's officials mainly kept their positions.<sup about="#mwt361" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERyan2013342_108-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERyan2013342&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERyan2013342-108&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ryan&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2013&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;342&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERyan2013342-108" id="mwAow"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAo0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAo4">[</span>96<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAo8">]</span></span></a></sup> Williams challenges the consensus that Edward's death was the result of a plot. She compares his death to [an earlier outbreak of violence due to a misunderstanding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaduheard "Beaduheard"):

> It is possible that King Edward's death was also 'accidental' in that, far from being planned, it arose from a provocative confrontation between the young king (prone to violent behaviour, according to Byrhtferth) and one or more of the noblemen attending on his brother. It remains curious that the perpetrator is not named; Edward's grandfather King Edmund was killed in similar circumstances but "it was widely known how he ended his life, that Leofa stabbed (*ofstang*) him at [Pucklechurch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pucklechurch "Pucklechurch")". Could it be that too many people (perhaps not all of them belonging to Ælfthryth's faction) were secretly relieved to be rid of a violent and unstable youth?<sup about="#mwt366" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200312_109-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200312&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200312-109&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200312-109" id="mwApM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApU">[</span>97<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApY">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Legacy

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Legacy")\]

### Burial and translation

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Burial and translation")\]

Edward's death deeply troubled contemporaries. Roach observes "Medieval kings were felt to be touched by divinity; not only had they been chosen by God, but like bishops they were anointed into their office with holy oil. Royal consecration was a well-established tradition and the reformers had done much to emphasize the [God-given nature of royal authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_right_of_kings "Divine right of kings") further ... To kill a king was, therefore, more than a crime – it was a sin of the first order."<sup about="#mwt368" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201674–75_110-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201674–75&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201674–75-110&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roach&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;74–75&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201674–75-110" id="mwAps"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAp0">[</span>98<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAp4">]</span></span></a></sup> England had a long tradition of revering murdered kings as saints, and the circumstances of Edward's death made it almost certain that he would come to be seen as a martyr, but this did not occur immediately. He was not regarded as saintly in his lifetime and he did not die defending [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity"). It was almost a year before he received royal burial, and the delay suggests that there was an argument over what to do with his body, with the case for honourable burial gradually gaining ground.<sup about="#mwt371" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Marafioti2014162–164_111-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Marafioti2014162–164&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Marafioti2014162–164-111&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;14&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Marafioti&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;162–164&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Marafioti2014162–164-111" id="mwAqA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqI">[</span>99<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqM">]</span></span></a></sup> *ASC D* and *ASC E* say that Edward was initially buried at Wareham without royal honour.<sup about="#mwt374" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231_112-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231-112&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;230–231&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979230–231-112" id="mwAqY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqg">[</span>100<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqk">]</span></span></a></sup> Byrhtferth states that a year later "the renowned Ealdorman Ælfhere arrived with a great train"; he ordered Edward's body to be disinterred, and it was found to be [incorrupt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incorrupt "Incorrupt"), which was taken as a miraculous sign; the body was carried with great ceremony for burial in [Shaftesbury Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaftesbury_Abbey "Shaftesbury Abbey"), a house of nuns.<sup about="#mwt377" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014162Lapidge2009140–143_113-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014162Lapidge2009140–143&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014162Lapidge2009140–143-113&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Marafioti&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;162&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;140–143&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014162Lapidge2009140–143-113" id="mwAqw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAq0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq4">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq8">]</span></span></a></sup> As the senior ealdorman, Ælfhere was probably charged with arranging the [translation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Translation_\(relic\) "Translation (relic)") of Edward's body from [Wareham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wareham,_Dorset "Wareham, Dorset") to [Shaftesbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaftesbury "Shaftesbury") for a proper burial to pave the way for Æthelred's coronation on 4 May.<sup about="#mwt380" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes199949_114-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes199949&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199949-114&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;49&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199949-114" id="mwArM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArU">[</span>102<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArY">]</span></span></a></sup> The early eleventh century [Archbishop of York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_York "Archbishop of York"), [Wulfstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wulfstan_\(died_1023\) "Wulfstan (died 1023)"), wrote that his body was burned, and Keynes comments that "the authority of Wulfstan is considerable",<sup about="#mwt383" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980167_115-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes1980167&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980167-115&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;167&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980167-115" id="mwArk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAro"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArs">[</span>103<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArw">]</span></span></a></sup> but as political circumstances required that Edward be decently buried, Ælfhere may have been charged with finding another body for the purpose.<sup about="#mwt386" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a124Whitelock1979931_116-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a124Whitelock1979931&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a124Whitelock1979931-116&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012a&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;124&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;931&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a124Whitelock1979931-116" id="mwAr0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAr4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAr8">[</span>104<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsA">]</span></span></a></sup>

Post-Conquest accounts, such as the *Passio,* have more complicated and hagiographical narratives. The *Passio* says that Ælfthryth had Edward's body concealed in a marsh, where it was miraculously revealed in February 979 by a column of fire, and locals took it to Wareham church for burial. Ælfhere was filled with joy when he heard about the discovery of the body, and he took it to Shaftesbury for reburial in a more worthy place.<sup about="#mwt389" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014168–169Ridyard1988155_117-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014168–169Ridyard1988155&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014168–169Ridyard1988155-117&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Marafioti&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;168–169&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ridyard&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;155&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarafioti2014168–169Ridyard1988155-117" id="mwAsQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsY">[</span>105<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsc">]</span></span></a></sup> The *Passio* goes on to say that in 1001 Edward told "a certain religious" in a vision of his wish to be moved from the churchyard to a more secure place in the abbey. This information was conveyed to the abbess, who passed it to King Æthelred, and he joyfully gave instructions for the relics to be again [translated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Translation_\(relics\) "Translation (relics)"). He ordered [Wulfsige](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wulfsige_III "Wulfsige III"), the [Bishop of Sherborne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Sherborne "Bishop of Sherborne"), and another prelate, perhaps Ælfsige, the abbot of the [New Minster, Winchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Minster,_Winchester "New Minster, Winchester"), to carry out the ceremony. The translation took place on 20 June, but the King could not be present because he was preoccupied with a Danish invasion.<sup about="#mwt392" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERidyard1988156Williams200315–16Foot2000170_118-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERidyard1988156Williams200315–16Foot2000170&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988156Williams200315–16Foot2000170-118&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ridyard&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;156&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;15–16&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Foot&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2000&quot;},&quot;3p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;170&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988156Williams200315–16Foot2000170-118" id="mwAs0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAs4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs8">[</span>106<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtA">]</span></span></a></sup> However, the historian Paul Hayward points out that this second translation is not mentioned in contemporary sources and no pre-Conquest calendar prescribes a feast on 20 June; he argues that it was an invention.<sup about="#mwt395" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHayward199986–87_119-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHayward199986–87&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHayward199986–87-119&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Hayward&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;86–87&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHayward199986–87-119" id="mwAtE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtM">[</span>107<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtQ">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Early cult

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Early cult")\]

Edward was recognised as a saint soon after his death, and Æthelred appears to have been the chief sponsor of his brother's [cult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veneration "Veneration").<sup about="#mwt398" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314_120-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200314&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314-120&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;14&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314-120" id="mwAtg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAto">[</span>108<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAts">]</span></span></a></sup> It was promoted initially by the ecclesiastical and secular leadership to demonstrate the sanctity of the royal office, but as Viking raids intensified they came to be seen as punishment of the English people by God for a terrible crime, the killing of the Lord's anointed, for which they needed to make amends.<sup about="#mwt401" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a125_121-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a125&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a125-121&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012a&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;125&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a125-121" id="mwAtw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAt0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt4">[</span>109<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt8">]</span></span></a></sup> [Sigeric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigeric_\(archbishop\) "Sigeric (archbishop)"), Archbishop of Canterbury from 990 to 994, persuaded Æthelred to establish a monastery at [Cholsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholsey_Abbey "Cholsey Abbey") in honour of Edward, and the king appointed [Germanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germanus_of_Winchester "Germanus of Winchester") as abbot. Edward's cult was also recognised at Canterbury and promoted by Wulfstan.<sup about="#mwt404" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a119,_122_122-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a119, 122&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a119,_122-122&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012a&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;119, 122&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes2012a119,_122-122" id="mwAuM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuU">[</span>110<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuY">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1001, Æthelred granted a former minster church and land at [Bradford on Avon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradford_on_Avon "Bradford on Avon") in Wiltshire to God and "his saint, my brother Edward, whom, drenched with his own blood, the Lord has seen fit to magnify in our time with many miracles", to provide a refuge for the nuns and Edward's relics against Viking attack.<sup about="#mwt407" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200316Ridyard1988156–157Charter_S_899_123-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200316Ridyard1988156–157Charter S 899&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200316Ridyard1988156–157Charter_S_899-123&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;16&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ridyard&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;156–157&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Charter S 899&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200316Ridyard1988156–157Charter_S_899-123" id="mwAug"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuo">[</span>111<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAus">]</span></span></a></sup> [Sarah Foot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Foot "Sarah Foot") describes the donation as "somewhat bizarre" as the Shaftesbury nunnery was in a fortified [burh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burh "Burh"), whereas Bradford was unprotected.<sup about="#mwt410" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFoot2000170–171Kelly1996119–120_124-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFoot2000170–171Kelly1996119–120&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoot2000170–171Kelly1996119–120-124&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Foot&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2000&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;170–171&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Kelly&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1996&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;119–120&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoot2000170–171Kelly1996119–120-124" id="mwAu4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAu8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvA">[</span>112<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvE">]</span></span></a></sup> Byrhtferth says that the first miracles occurred at Edward's tomb eleven years after his death.<sup about="#mwt413" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge2009144–145Williams200315_125-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge2009144–145Williams200315&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009144–145Williams200315-125&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lapidge&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2009&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;144–145&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;15&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge2009144–145Williams200315-125" id="mwAvI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvQ">[</span>113<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvU">]</span></span></a></sup>

Wulfstan's *[Sermon of the Wolf to the English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sermon_of_the_Wolf_to_the_English "Sermon of the Wolf to the English")* of around 1014 cites the murder of Edward and Æthelred being forced into exile by [Sweyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweyn_Forkbeard "Sweyn Forkbeard")'s conquest of England in 1013 as examples of betrayals of lords by the English.<sup about="#mwt416" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams2003122–123Whitelock1979931_126-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams2003122–123Whitelock1979931&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2003122–123Whitelock1979931-126&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;122–123&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Whitelock&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1979&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;931&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2003122–123Whitelock1979931-126" id="mwAvo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvw">[</span>114<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv0">]</span></span></a></sup> Out of twenty-five calendars dating to before 1100, Edward's death date of 18 March is listed in eighteen;
