only nine saints were listed in more, and five also have a feast on 13 February commemorating his translation from Wareham to Shaftesbury.<sup about="#mwt419" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatson202110–11Rollason1989142–143_127-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWatson202110–11Rollason1989142–143&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson202110–11Rollason1989142–143-127&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Watson&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10–11&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rollason&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1989&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;142–143&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson202110–11Rollason1989142–143-127" id="mwAv4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAv8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwA">[</span>115<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwE">]</span></span></a></sup> Æthelred's eldest son, [Æthelstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelstan_Ætheling "Æthelstan Ætheling") left £6 "to Holy Cross and St Edward at Shaftesbury" in his will, made on his deathbed in 1014. Cnut also patronised Edward's cult, and the law code V Æthelred of 1008 includes a clause, possibly inserted by [Cnut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cnut "Cnut") in around 1018, which commands that the feast of Edward's martyrdom be celebrated on 18 March throughout England.<sup about="#mwt422" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Thacker1996267Wormald197853–54Keynes199953,_70_n._130Keynes2012a123_128-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Thacker1996267Wormald197853–54Keynes199953, 70 n. 130Keynes2012a123&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Thacker1996267Wormald197853–54Keynes199953,_70_n._130Keynes2012a123-128&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Williams&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;14&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Thacker&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1996&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;267&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Wormald&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1978&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;53–54&quot;},&quot;4a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;4y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;4pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;53, 70 n. 130&quot;},&quot;5a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;5y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012a&quot;},&quot;5p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;123&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams200314Thacker1996267Wormald197853–54Keynes199953,_70_n._130Keynes2012a123-128" id="mwAwQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwY">[</span>116<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwc">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian [David Rollason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rollason "David Rollason") argues that Æthelred and Cnut promoted Edward's cult in order to heighten their prestige by emphasising the sanctity of their predecessor.<sup about="#mwt425" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERollason1989144_129-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERollason1989144&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERollason1989144-129&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rollason&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1989&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;144&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERollason1989144-129" id="mwAwk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAws">[</span>117<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAww">]</span></span></a></sup> The killing of Edward led to greater interest in other murdered royal saints by Oswald and in his monasteries, especially Ramsey and [Winchcombe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winchcombe_Abbey "Winchcombe Abbey").<sup about="#mwt428" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECubitt200067_130-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTECubitt200067&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTECubitt200067-130&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Cubitt&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2000&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;67&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECubitt200067-130" id="mwAw4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAw8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxA">[</span>118<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxE">]</span></span></a></sup>

Æthelred's failure as a king has been seen by post-Conquest writers and some modern historians as a result of Edward's murder. [Goscelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goscelin "Goscelin") wrote in his life of Edith that Æthelred was unworthy to rule because "his succession had been purchased with his brother's blood".<sup about="#mwt431" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERidyard1988158_131-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERidyard1988158&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988158-131&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ridyard&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;158&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988158-131" id="mwAxQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxY">[</span>119<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxc">]</span></span></a></sup> Stenton comments that Æthelred "began to reign in an atmosphere of suspicion which destroyed the prestige of the Crown. It was never fully restored in his lifetime ... Much that has brought the condemnation of historians on King Æthelred may well be due in the last resort to the circumstances under which he became king." His ineffective conduct as king suggests "the reaction of a weak king to the consciousness that he had come to power through what his subjects regarded as the worst crime committed among the English peoples since their first coming to Britain".<sup about="#mwt434" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStenton1971373–374_132-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStenton1971373–374&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971373–374-132&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stenton&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;373–374&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStenton1971373–374-132" id="mwAxg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxo">[</span>120<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxs">]</span></span></a></sup> Æthelred's support for his brother's cult has been seen as an attempt to dispel the cloud of suspicion which hung over him, but this view has been challenged since the late twentieth century by historians who have argued that contemporaries do not appear to have blamed Æthelred or his mother for the murder.<sup about="#mwt437" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERidyard1988159–162_133-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERidyard1988159–162&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988159–162-133&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ridyard&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1988&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;159–162&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERidyard1988159–162-133" id="mwAxw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAx0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAx4">[</span>121<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAx8">]</span></span></a></sup> Keynes argues that if the royal family had been blamed for Edward's death, glorification of him would have drawn attention to the crime and undermined Æthelred's position.<sup about="#mwt440" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980171_134-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes1980171&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980171-134&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1980&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;171&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes1980171-134" id="mwAyA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyI">[</span>122<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyM">]</span></span></a></sup>

Several religious communities claimed to have acquired parts of Edward's body during Cnut's reign, but William of Malmesbury stated that half of Edward's body was taken to [Leominster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leominster_Abbey "Leominster Abbey") and half to Abingdon, where they both crumbled, and only the lung remained at Shaftesbury, where it was displayed, continuing to throb.<sup about="#mwt443" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBugyis2019225_and_n._1Winterbottom2007294–297_(ii.86.6)Marafioti2014214–215_135-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEBugyis2019225 and n. 1Winterbottom2007294–297 (ii.86.6)Marafioti2014214–215&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBugyis2019225_and_n._1Winterbottom2007294–297_(ii.86.6)Marafioti2014214–215-135&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Bugyis&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2019&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;225 and n. 1&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Winterbottom&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2007&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;294–297 (ii.86.6)&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Marafioti&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2014&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;214–215&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBugyis2019225_and_n._1Winterbottom2007294–297_(ii.86.6)Marafioti2014214–215-135" id="mwAyY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyg">[</span>123<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyk">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Later cult

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Later cult")\]

Edward was the only tenth century king to be buried in a nunnery. Shaftesbury, which had been founded by Alfred the Great for one of his daughters, had strong royal connections,<sup about="#mwt446" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke202168_136-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke202168&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke202168-136&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;68&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke202168-136" id="mwAyw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAy0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAy4">[</span>124<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAy8">]</span></span></a></sup> and the cult of Edward was valuable to it, giving it a high status among Wessex monasteries. At the end of the Anglo-Saxon period it was the richest Benedictine nunnery and Glastonbury the richest monastery of all. Later in the Middle Ages there was a saying that "If the abbot of Glastonbury might marry the abbess of Shaftesbury, their heir would have more land than the King of England".<sup about="#mwt449" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955–56_137-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes199955–56&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955–56-137&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;55–56&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955–56-137" id="mwAzA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzI">[</span>125<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzM">]</span></span></a></sup> Edward's cult was important for the prosperity of the nunnery and town of Shaftesbury in the later Middle Ages, and in some medieval documents the town is called *Edwardsstowe*, "the holy place of Edward".<sup about="#mwt452" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke199999_138-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke199999&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke199999-138&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;99&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke199999-138" id="mwAzU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzc">[</span>126<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzg">]</span></span></a></sup>

[Lanfranc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lanfranc "Lanfranc"), the first Norman Archbishop of Canterbury, denied the sanctity of many Anglo-Saxon saints. Edward's cult survived but it was regarded as "rustic" and relegated to a minor status only to be honoured in establishments with a particular reason to honour him, such as Shaftesbury. His cult revived in the later Middle Ages, although almost wholly in the southern half of the country. He was regarded as one of the English national saints until they were relegated by the Plantagenets' preference for the more martial figure of [Saint George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_George "Saint George"). Edward survived the [English Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Reformation "English Reformation"), but as a low key figure only remembered on his feast day.<sup about="#mwt455" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatson20219–10,_14–17_139-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWatson20219–10, 14–17&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20219–10,_14–17-139&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Watson&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;9–10, 14–17&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20219–10,_14–17-139" id="mwAz0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAz4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAz8">[</span>127<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0A">]</span></span></a></sup> Edward's feast of 18 March is still listed in the festal calendar of the [1662 *Book of Common Prayer*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Common_Prayer_\(1662\) "Book of Common Prayer (1662)") of the [Church of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_England "Church of England").<sup about="#mwt458" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatson20211_140-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWatson20211&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20211-140&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Watson&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson20211-140" id="mwA0Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0Y">[</span>128<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0c">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian [Frank Barlow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Barlow_\(historian\) "Frank Barlow (historian)") comments that popular detestation of the crime led people to transform an unpleasant youth into a royal martyr.<sup about="#mwt461" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBarlow19974_141-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEBarlow19974&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarlow19974-141&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Barlow&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1997&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;4&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarlow19974-141" id="mwA0k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0s">[</span>129<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0w">]</span></span></a></sup> The historian Tom Watson commented, "For an obnoxious teenager who showed no evidence of sanctity or kingly attributes and who should have been barely a footnote, his cult has endured mightily well."<sup about="#mwt464" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatson202119_142-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWatson202119&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson202119-142&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Watson&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;19&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatson202119-142" id="mwA00"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA04"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA08">[</span>130<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1A">]</span></span></a></sup>

### Dispute over Edward's bones

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Dispute over Edward's bones")\]

Shaftesbury Abbey was [dissolved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_monasteries "Dissolution of the monasteries") in 1539 and the buildings were almost wholly dismantled. Between 1930 and 1932, an amateur historian, John Wilson-Claridge, conducted excavations in the ruins of the abbey. No report of the excavations was ever published, but he claimed to have found the bones of Edward the Martyr in the north transept. The discovery was praised in *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times "The Times")* as one of the greatest historical discoveries of the century.<sup about="#mwt467" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeen19995–7Parlby200337_143-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeen19995–7Parlby200337&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeen19995–7Parlby200337-143&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keen&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;5–7&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Parlby&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2003&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;37&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeen19995–7Parlby200337-143" id="mwA1Y"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1c"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1g">[</span>131<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1k">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1963, the bones were examined by the forensic pathologist [Thomas Stowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_E._A._Stowell "Thomas E. A. Stowell"), who concluded that they were of a young man between the ages of seventeen and nineteen who had suffered injuries consistent with the description of the murder in Byrhtferth's account. Stowell concluded that beyond reasonable doubt the bones were of Edward the Martyr.<sup about="#mwt470" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStowell1971160Lavelle200844_144-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStowell1971160Lavelle200844&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStowell1971160Lavelle200844-144&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stowell&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;160&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lavelle&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;44&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStowell1971160Lavelle200844-144" id="mwA1s"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA10">[</span>132<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA14">]</span></span></a></sup> One of the bones was carbon-dated and found to date to the correct period.<sup about="#mwt473" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955,_70_n._132_145-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes199955, 70 n. 132&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955,_70_n._132-145&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;55, 70 n. 132&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199955,_70_n._132-145" id="mwA18"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2E">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2I">]</span></span></a></sup> Historians' accounts of Stowell's findings usually mention that they were contradicted by the British Museum osteoarchaeologist [Don Brothwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Brothwell "Don Brothwell"), who is believed to have examined the bones and concluded that they were of an older man and that the damage was probably post-mortem, although no report of his examination was ever published.<sup about="#mwt476" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Rahtz198917Gem198411_146-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Rahtz198917Gem198411&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Rahtz198917Gem198411-146&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;54–55&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rahtz&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1989&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;17&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Gem&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1984&quot;},&quot;3p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Rahtz198917Gem198411-146" id="mwA2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2Y">[</span>134<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2c">]</span></span></a></sup>

Wilson-Claridge negotiated with several churches to take the bones, but none was willing to agree to his requirement that they would be housed and revered as the true relics of the saint.<sup about="#mwt479" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStowell1971141_147-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStowell1971141&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStowell1971141-147&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Stowell&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1971&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;141&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStowell1971141-147" id="mwA2k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2s">[</span>135<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2w">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1980, Wilson-Claridge met, in his own words, "by divine providence" a Mr Pobjoy, who was a member of the [Russian Orthodox Church Abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church_Abroad "Russian Orthodox Church Abroad") (ROCA), which agreed to accept the bones on Wilson-Claridge's terms.<sup about="#mwt482" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERahtz198917_148-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERahtz198917&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERahtz198917-148&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rahtz&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1989&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;17&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERahtz198917-148" id="mwA24"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA28"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3A">[</span>136<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3E">]</span></span></a></sup> Wilson-Claridge's brother strongly objected to the bones going to ROCA, arguing that they should go back to Shaftesbury. The dispute was debated in letters to *The Times*, including one from Keynes which objected to the bones going to a Russian Orthodox church: "No Saxon can have deserved that fate". In 1988, a High Court hearing ruled that the bones could go to the ROCA [Church of St Edward the Martyr, Brookwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St_Edward_the_Martyr,_Brookwood "Church of St Edward the Martyr, Brookwood"), which had been established for the purpose.<sup about="#mwt485" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERahtz198917Keynes198411_149-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERahtz198917Keynes198411&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERahtz198917Keynes198411-149&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Rahtz&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1989&quot;},&quot;1p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;17&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1984&quot;},&quot;2p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;11&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERahtz198917Keynes198411-149" id="mwA3Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA3U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3Y">[</span>137<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3c">]</span></span></a></sup> Historians are very sceptical that the bones in the Brookwood church are those of Edward the Martyr, both because the body taken to Shaftesbury in 979 was probably not Edward's, and because the bones found in 1931 were probably not the ones believed by contemporaries to be his.<sup about="#mwt488" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Yorke1999112–113Watson202117–18_150-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Yorke1999112–113Watson202117–18&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeynes199954–55Yorke1999112–113Watson202117–18-150&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfnm&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfnm&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Keynes&quot;},&quot;1y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;1pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;54–55&quot;},&quot;2a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Yorke&quot;},&quot;2y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1999&quot;},&quot;2pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;112–113&quot;},&quot;3a1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Watson&quot;},&quot;3y&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2021&quot;},&quot;3pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;17–18&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3s">]</span></span></a></sup>

## Notes

\[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Edward_the_Martyr&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Notes")\]

1. [↑](#cite_ref-2 "Jump up") A charter's S number is its number in [Peter Sawyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Sawyer_\(historian\) "Peter Sawyer (historian)")'s catalogue of Anglo-Saxon charters, available online at the [Electronic Sawyer](https://esawyer.lib.cam.ac.uk/about/index.html).
2. [↑](#cite_ref-7 "Jump up") Fell and Ridyard think that the *Passio* was probably written by Goscelin, but Paul Hayward considers this "highly unlikely", arguing that "the work has little of the sweeping flair and distinctive vocabulary which characterises Goscelin's oeuvre".<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFell1971xxRidyard198848–49Hayward199985–86_and_n._85_6-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEFell1971xxRidyard198848–49Hayward199985–86 and n. 85&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xxRidyard198848–49Hayward199985–86_and_n._85-6&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFell1971xxRidyard198848–49Hayward199985–86_and_n._85-6" id="mwA7E"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA7I"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA7M">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA7Q">]</span></span></a></sup>
3. [↑](#cite_ref-18 "Jump up") Ealdorman was the second rank of the lay aristocracy below the king. They governed large areas as the king's local representatives and led local levies in battle.<sup about="#mwt64" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStafford2014156_17-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStafford2014156&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2014156-17&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford2014156-17" id="mwA7g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA7k"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA7o">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA7s">]</span></span></a></sup>
4. [↑](#cite_ref-20 "Jump up") Æthelstan was known as the Half-King because kings were said to rely on his advice. He retired in 957 and was succeeded as ealdorman of East Anglia by his eldest son, [Æthelwold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwald,_Ealdorman_of_East_Anglia "Æthelwald, Ealdorman of East Anglia"), Ælfthryth's first husband, who died in 962. Æthelwold was succeeded as ealdorman by his youngest brother, [Æthelwine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Æthelwine,_Ealdorman_of_East_Anglia "Æthelwine, Ealdorman of East Anglia").<sup about="#mwt70" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiller2014d19Lapidge200984–87Lapidge201420_19-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEMiller2014d19Lapidge200984–87Lapidge201420&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014d19Lapidge200984–87Lapidge201420-19&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller2014d19Lapidge200984–87Lapidge201420-19" id="mwA8E"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA8I"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8M">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8Q">]</span></span></a></sup>
5. [↑](#cite_ref-29 "Jump up") The twelfth century *[Liber Eliensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liber_Eliensis "Liber Eliensis")* states that Edgar later claimed that Ordmær and Ealde bequeathed the land at Hatfield to him and gave the Hatfield land to Ely Abbey. After his death Æthelstan's sons claimed that Edgar had forced him to surrender it. They brought a successful action to recover the land from the abbey.<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHart1992462–463,_586Fairweather2005103–104_28-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEHart1992462–463, 586Fairweather2005103–104&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992462–463,_586Fairweather2005103–104-28&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHart1992462–463,_586Fairweather2005103–104-28" id="mwA8o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA8s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8w">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA80">]</span></span></a></sup>
6. [↑](#cite_ref-31 "Jump up") [Ann Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Williams_\(historian\) "Ann Williams (historian)") disputes the consensus, suggesting that Edward was a son of Wulfthryth and thus a full brother of Edith. She thinks that Æthelflæd was invented to absolve Edgar from Osbern's charge that he seduced a nun.<sup about="#mwt103" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20036,_159_n._33_30-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20036, 159 n. 33&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20036,_159_n._33-30&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20036,_159_n._33-30" id="mwA9I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA9M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9Q">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9U">]</span></span></a></sup>
7. [↑](#cite_ref-38 "Jump up") Historians do not agree on Wulfthryth's status. [Rosalind Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosalind_Love "Rosalind Love") refers to her as a concubine,<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELove2014154_36-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELove2014154&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELove2014154-36&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELove2014154-36" id="mwA9o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA9s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9w">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA90">]</span></span></a></sup> whereas her status as wife is accepted by [Pauline Stafford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauline_Stafford "Pauline Stafford") and defended by [Barbara Yorke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Yorke "Barbara Yorke").<sup about="#mwt126" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStafford198951Yorke200397–113_37-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEStafford198951Yorke200397–113&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford198951Yorke200397–113-37&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStafford198951Yorke200397–113-37" id="mwA-A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA-E"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA-I">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA-M">]</span></span></a></sup>
8. [↑](#cite_ref-48 "Jump up") In his will, Ælfhere's brother Ælfheah described Ælfthryth as his *gefædera*, a word which denotes the relationship between a parent and a godparent or godparents of the same child. He made bequests to Ælfthryth and her sons, "the elder ætheling" and "the younger ætheling". Edward is not mentioned.<sup about="#mwt160" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams20032,_9Whitelock193022–23_47-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWilliams20032, 9Whitelock193022–23&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20032,_9Whitelock193022–23-47&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20032,_9Whitelock193022–23-47" id="mwA-g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA-k"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA-o">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA-s">]</span></span></a></sup>
9. [↑](#cite_ref-59 "Jump up") Manuscripts of the *Anglo-Saxon Chronicle* are conventionally labelled *ASC A* to *ASC F*.<sup about="#mwt202" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979109–117_58-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979109–117&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979109–117-58&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979109–117-58" id="mwA_I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_Q">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_U">]</span></span></a></sup>
10. [↑](#cite_ref-87 "Jump up") The historian [David Dumville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Dumville "David Dumville") argues that Edward was killed on 18 March 979, but most historians give 978,<sup about="#mwt290" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDumville2007269–283Keynes2012b137Marafioti2014162_n._3_85-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEDumville2007269–283Keynes2012b137Marafioti2014162 n. 3&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007269–283Keynes2012b137Marafioti2014162_n._3-85&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumville2007269–283Keynes2012b137Marafioti2014162_n._3-85" id="mwA_o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_w">[</span>76<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_0">]</span></span></a></sup> and Levi Roach thinks that there is little doubt that 978 is correct.<sup about="#mwt292" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoach201672–73_86-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTERoach201672–73&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201672–73-86&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoach201672–73-86" id="mwA_4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAA">[</span>77<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAE">]</span></span></a></sup>
11. [↑](#cite_ref-91 "Jump up") The [medieval Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_Latin "Medieval Latin") scholar [Michael Lapidge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Lapidge "Michael Lapidge") argues that parallels between the biblical description of the betrayal of Christ and Byrhtferth's account of Edward's death suggest that he may have fabricated it,<sup about="#mwt302" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELapidge199679–80_89-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTELapidge199679–80&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge199679–80-89&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELapidge199679–80-89" id="mwBAc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBAg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAk">[</span>79<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAo">]</span></span></a></sup> but Yorke disagrees, and Roach sees little reason not to trust Byrhtferth.<sup about="#mwt304" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke1999102Roach201677_90-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEYorke1999102Roach201677&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999102Roach201677-90&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke1999102Roach201677-90" id="mwBAs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBAw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBA0">[</span>80<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBA4">]</span></span></a></sup>
12. [↑](#cite_ref-93 "Jump up") *ASC D* and *E* are together known as the northern recension because they contain material of northern interest not found in other versions.<sup about="#mwt311" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979113_92-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979113&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979113-92&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWhitelock1979113-92" id="mwBBQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBBU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBBY">[</span>81<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBBc">]</span></span></a></sup>
